Article 36. For the purpose of determining net taxable income, all classified new industries, in addition to the items deductible from profits by virtue of article 39 of the Income Tax Law, may deduct from the profits of 1 year the losses suffered in earlier years, under the following terms and limitations: a. Industries classified in category "A" may deduct from the profits of 1 tax year, all or a part of the losses suffered in as many as the 4 prior tax years. Such industries are eligible to make use of this right during the first 10 years of the operation of the enterprise; b. Industries classified in category "B" may take these deductions under terms similar to those fixed in the preceding subsection, except with regard to the period during which they might use this right, which shall embrace only the first 7 years of the operation of the enterprise; c. Industries classified in category "C" may deduct from the profits of 1 tax year, all or part of the losses for as many as the 2 prior years. Such industries are eligible to make use of this right during the first 5 years of the operation of the enterprise.

The Ministry of the Treasury will regulate the procedures which must be followed for the implementation of this article, being empowered to resolve the unforeseen cases which originate therefrom.

Article 37.-For the application of articles 35 and 36, "years of operation" shall be understood as the number of years which the corresponding enterprise is in effective production. If effective production of the enterprise is initiated during the first 9 months of the calendar year, the period of time which elapses from the date of this beginning until the end of the corresponding calendar year shall be considered as the first complete year of operation. If effective production begins during the last 

3 months of the year, the following calendar year will be considered as the first of operation.

Article 38.-For the purposes of this law, the formal beginning of the activity, subsequent to a merger of industrial enterprises, its splitting, or the sale, cession, transfer, or mere change of the denomination or firm name of the industry, will not constitute an initiation of the effective production of an enterprise.

In the case of a merger of enterprises, the number of years of operation of the new enterprise will be the same as that of the oldest enterprise of those that have merged. In the case of a splitting of an enterprise, the number of years of operation of the new industries will be added to those of the original enterprise. In the case of a sale, cession, transfer, or mere change of the denomination or firm name of an enterprise, the number of years of operation of the industry sold, ceded, transferred, or of that whose activity was carried on under the original denomination or firm name will be taken into account.

When some doubt exists, the Ministries of Economy and of the Treasury, attentive to the national interest, shall decide if a merger or division of enterprises has taken place, or if the sale, cession, transfer, or mere change of the denomination or firm name of an enterprise has been verified, and, if appropriate, the oldest enterprise of those which have merged. 

In the event of bankruptcy or liquidation of an enterprise, duly notified to the Ministry of Economy, the Ministry and the Treasury shall decide if the commencement of activity of the acquiring enterprises constitutes the initiation of its effective production.

Article 39.-When industrial enterprises which function in national territory establish affiliate enterprises, subsidiaries, or any kind of enterprises which maintain a financial or administrative relation with them, the Ministries of Economy and Treasury, considering the characteristics of their activity, shall decide, in each case, if they may be considered as new industries and if their commencement of activity constitutes the initiation of effective production, for the purposes of the tax concessions under articles 35 and 36 of this law. 

As a basis for their decision, the aforementioned Ministries must examine principally whether the lines of production of these dependent or related enterprises are different from those to which the parent or principal industry is dedicated and, when such is not the case, if the establishment of such enterprises is justified for technical reasons and is advantageous to the national economic interest.

Article 40.-The Ministry of Economy shall qualify the industrial enterprises as efficient or not efficient for the purposes of the periodic reconsideration of the tax reductions. This qualification must be done in the last trimester of the year in which reconsideration is required, in conformity with the scale of article 35 of this law. 

The Ministry of the Treasury, in conformity with the qualification made by the respective Ministerial Accord, shall notify the interested party as to the reductions which will be enjoyed in the period immediately following according to the corresponding scale. 

Article 41.-The Ministry of Economy, through the Directorate General of Industries and Commerce (Direccion 

General de Industrias y Comercio), upon application of the interested enterprise, shall carry out the necessary inspections in order to determine the technical and admin- 155

istrative efficiency of the enterprise; taking into account, on one hand, the cooperation which shall be given to the economic and social policies of the state and, on the other hand, the degree of coincidence in its operation of the criteria which, in order of importance, are listed below: a. The ingenuity of the enterprise to take care of the requirements of the consumer, especially with regard to the improvement of the quality of the product or products, or an increase in production; b. The utilization of industrial equipment which incorporates the maximum degree of technical progress consistent with the size and other characteristics of the market; c. The following of a price policy which harmonizes, insofar as possible, the legitimate interests of the entrepreneur with the aspirations of the consumer; d. The preoccupation of the enterprise in improving the welfare of its employees and its faithful observance of the 

Labor Law; e. The increase in the proportion of national raw material and semimanufactured products used in its production; f. The contribution of the enterprise to the technical and professional training of personnel, whether it be independently or in collaboration with other enterprises, entities, or the state; g. The efforts of the industry in favor of technical research either in the enterprise itself or outside; h. The use of modern methods of accounting, especially of costs; i. Its contribution to the use of national hand labor and the technical efficiency of its personnel; and j. The importance of the investment. 

Article 42. The regulations shall determine the norms to be followed in order to classify the efficiency of the industry in conformity with the criteria enunciated in the previous article, but in no case may an industry be classified as efficient if its operation does not conform to the criteria established in paragraph (e) when it is technically and economically possible to substitute national products for imported raw material or semimanufactured products being used. That provided in paragraph (h) shall likewise not be ignored. 

Article 43.-When the benefits of a tax nature, granted to a new enterprise as a result of article 35 of this law, are the basis of proven ruinous competition for some existing enterprises which produce similar or directly substitutable articles, the Ministries of Economy and of the Treasury, upon application of the prejudiced enterprise, presented with the support of the Superior Council of 

Chambers of Industry (Consejo Superior de Camaras de 

Industrias) and with the prior favorable report of the

National Board of Economic Planning and Coordination, may grant to that enterprise reductions in the taxes on capital and on sales, in conformity with the rules established in the following article.

In no case may the complaining enterprise present the application, referred to in the previous paragraph, before the end of 1 year after the initiation of effective production by the new enterprise and it may only be done if the following conditions are met: a. That it be a classified industrial enterprise or be in a condition to acquire this classification in accordance with that provided in this law. In the latter case, the 

Ministerial Accord must signify the category of the industry in order to proceed to fix the tax reductions; b. That it be classified as an efficient industry by the 

Ministry of Economy in compliance with that provided in article 40; c. That it prove to the satisfaction of the Ministries of 

Economy and of the Treasury and of the National Board of Economic Planning and Coordination that the normal functioning of the enterprise is conditioned by the grant of a concession for tax reductions. 

Article 44. For the determination of the decreasing scales for reduction of the taxes on capital (capital en giro) and on sales which are to be granted to the complainant enterprise when its application is accepted, the Ministries of Economy and of the Treasury shall be guided by the necessity for eliminating the disadvantageous situation of that enterprise, but they shall be subject, in every case, to the following rules: a. The scale in article 35 corresponding to the category of the new enterprise shall serve as a base for the concession of the tax reductions; b. The scale of reduction must not embrace a greater number of years than that which remains to the new enterprise which is enjoying this protection and for its application it must in every case be considered that the complainant enterprise has the same number of years of operation as that of the new industry. The determination of the coefficients for reduction will be subject to the following limitations;

I. When the complainant enterprise is of a higher or equal category than the new enterprise, the coefficients for reduction may not be greater than 80 percent of the coefficients for reduction applied to the new enterprise; 

II. When the complainant enterprise is of a lesser category than the new enterprise, the coefficients for reduction may not be greater than 50 percent of the coefficients for reduction applied to the new enterprise.

Article 45.-The scale of tax reductions applicable to the complainant enterprise shall also be subject to that established in the basic scale with respect to the periodic reconsideration of the tax concessions, and the Ministry of Economy, in compliance with that provided in article 40 of this law, shall proceed, when appropriate, to classify the efficiency of the enterprise.

The classification of inefficiency shall be sufficient cause for making the complainant enterprise ineligible to continue the enjoyment of the tax reductions. 

Article 46.-When for any reason the enjoyment of the tax concessions made to a new enterprise in conformity with article 35 is suspended or withdrawn, totally or partially, it shall be tacitly understood that the regime of concessions applicable to the enterprise or enterprises which have obtained this protection against the competition of the first is suspended or withdrawn, totally or partially, in a corresponding manner. When it is a case of a lowering of the percentages of reduction of the taxes which apply to the new enterprise, the percentages of reduction which the complainant enjoys shall be lowered to the same amount.

Article 47.-The organization of share-issuing companies (sociedades por acciones) or general partnerships (sociedades de nombre colectivo) which serve as the vehicles for the activities of the transformation industries embraced in this law, may be attended by the following benefits: 

1. Total exoneration from the stamp tax on shares and a 50-percent reduction of the stamp tax on the constitutive document (escritura de constitucion) and of the registration tax. The registration shall be effected on a temporary basis through the presentation of the provisional authorization to which article 48 of this law refers, and shall have a definitive character if this authorization is not revoked by the Ministry of Economy during the term fixed by the Ministerial Accord for the initiation of the industry's effective production. 

2. Deferred payment, interest free, of the following taxes: a. Of 50 percent of the stamp tax on the constitutive document, the payment of which may be effected in four semiannual quotas, the first of which must be made at the end of the second year of operations of the industry, in conformity with the definition in article 37 of this law; b. Of the tax on capital (capital en giro) which must be reduced in conformity with the percentage indicated by article 35 of this law in the scale which corresponds 156

Industrial Encouragement Law to the category of the industry. The computation and collection of this tax shall be effected as soon as the issuance of the Ministerial Accord in which the category of the corresponding industrial enterprise is determined. 

Article 48. The promoters of share-issuing companies or of general partnerships organized for industrial purposes, who wish to enjoy the benefits provided in the previous article for the establishment of the company, must present, for this purpose, an application to the Ministry of Economy, in which they will provide, in addition to the name and other general information required by law of promoters, the characteristics of the company being organized, its probable capital, its proposed purposes, and the necessary data as to the industrial area which it intends to exploit.

The acceptance of the application by the Ministry of Economy and the resultant issuance of a provisional authorization for the establishment of the company will entitle the promoters to enjoy the benefits provided in the previous article of this law. 

Article 49.-If the promoters of industrial enterprises, in addition to enjoying the benefits pertaining to the organization of the company, wish to assure themselves with respect to the classification which will apply to the enterprise when they organize, the application treated in the previous article must be supplemented by the questionnaire to which article 16 of this law refers, and follow a procedure similar to that for applications for the establishment of an industry referred to in paragraph (a) of article 17 of this same law; but, in such event, if the application is accepted, the category of the industry will be indicated in the provisional authorization for the organization of the company, without the necessity of a Ministerial Accord, which only shall be issued confirming the provisional classification if there has not been a change in the characteristics of the enterprise when it is legally organized.

Article 50. The provisional authorization to which the second paragraph of article 48 refers shall be considered tacitly revoked if one or more of the following circumstances occur prior to the initiation of the effective production of the industry: 

1. If the company, already legally established, does not present the industrial development applications, to which reference is made in articles 16 and 17 of this law, within a term of 2 months after the date on which the provisional authorization was issued ; 

2. When the respective industrial development application is not accepted by the Ministries of Economy and of the Treasury and, consequently, the industry is not classified; 

3. If the enterprise has not begun effective production within the time provided for that purpose in the corresponding Ministerial Accord; and 

4. If it is proved that the established company is devoted to activities other than those declared in the promoters' application, when, in the judgment of the Ministry of Economy, the change in activity runs contra to the purpose of this Industrial Development Law, without prejudice to the sanctions provided for the case of willful misstatements in the declarations.

The revocation of the provisional authorization for the organization of the company, once notified to the Ministry of the Treasury or to the corresponding retention agents, constitutes authority to proceed with the immediate collection of the taxes on the organization of the company which were provisionally excused or deferred. 

The collection of interest and the period of default shall begin on the date of cancellation of the provisional authorization, except in cases of fraud, in which it shall be considered that the provisional authorization is null and void.

Article 51.-When the corresponding industry has begun its effective production and has not provided any grounds for the tacit revocation treated in the previous article, the provisional authorization shall have a definitive character for all legal purposes; but it may be annulled within the 5 years following the initiation of production, when the company is in the situation treated in sub section (4) of the previous article. The annulment of the definitive authorization constitutes authorization to proceed with the immediate collection of the taxes on the organization of the company which were excused or deferred in analogous terms to those prescribed in the last paragraph of the previous article for the revocation of the provisional authorization.

Article 52.-The reinvestment of the profits earned in the previous year or years and investment financed through credits obtained by industrial enterprises already established and classified, whatever the legal form of such enterprises, when utilized in additions or improvement of their equipment, through the acquisition of new machines or accessories and not the simple replacement of existing ones, may be partially deducted from the gross profits for the determination of taxable income, under the following terms: a. Industries classified in category "A" may deduct 50 percent of the aforementioned reinvestment or investment effected on credit; b. Industries classified in category "B" may deduct 35 percent of such reinvestment or investment; c. Industries classified in category "C" may deduct 25 percent of such reinvestment or investment. 

Under the same conditions as in the previous subsections of this article, when the increase or improvement of the machines or accessories is effected through an increase in the capital of the enterprise, 25 percent of such investment may be deducted from the gross profits of the tax year.

The provisions of this article are not in lieu of the other deductions which the industrial enterprises have the right to take, in conformity with subsection (j) of article 39 of the Income Tax Law, for the reinvestment of profits in assets other than machinery and accessories. 

Article 53. In addition to that provided in article 6 of the regulation for the application of subsection (j) of article 39 of the Income Tax Law with reference to the reinvestment of profits generally, for the purposes of the previous article there shall be considered as the amount of reinvestment, investment through credit, or increase in capital, solely that amount which is employed in the acquisition of the machinery and accessories the importation of which, completely or partially exempt from customs duties and consular fees, has been authorized in the corresponding Ministerial Accord, or the transfer of which, in the event of purchases within the country, has been known to and authorized by the Ministry of Economy.

In either case the Ministry of the Treasury shall require the presentation of a certificate issued by the Ministry of Economy as to the authenticity of the acquisition and the price at which it was verified. 

The Ministry of Economy may refuse this certificate when the declared prices for acquisition of the machinery and accessories obviously differ from a level which reasonably may be considered as normal, or when there exist bases for suspicion that the declared price is greater than that actually paid, even when it approximates the value which may be estimated as normal. 

Article 54.-If the machinery and accessories, for the acquisition of which profits have been reinvested or the purchase of which has been financed through borrowing or increase of capital, are sold with the authorization of the Ministry of Economy in the 2 years following that in which the corresponding deduction has been taken, the respective tax must be recomputed, under the following terms: a. If the sale is made within the year following that in which the deduction was taken, the enterprise must pay the amount of the difference between the income tax 157

which would have corresponded to it if the deduction had not been taken and the tax which was actually paid; b. If the sale is brought about during the second year following that in which the deduction occurred, the enterprise shall pay half of the difference between that actually collected as income tax and that which ought to have been paid without the corresponding deduction. 

Article 55.-The classified industrial enterprises are eligible to enjoy, through application to the Bureau of 

Income Tax (Direccion del Impuesto a la Renta), a special system of deductions for amortization of their machinery and accessories, in conformity with the following norms: a. Transformation industries of categories "A" and "B" may deduct annually from gross profits obtained the amount necessary to amortize in 5 years the value of their investments in machinery and accessories. As a result, they are eligible to deduct annually, during the 5 years, 20 percent of the value of these investments; b. Transformation industries of category "C" may deduct annually from gross profit obtained, the amount necessary to amortize in 8 years the value of their investments in machinery and accessories. As a result, they are eligible to deduct annually, during the 8 years, 12.5 percent of the value of these investments.

The prior provisions will not prevent the enterprise, if it so desires, from choosing the normal system of amortization established in the Income Tax Law, even when it has begun the amortization under the special system described earlier, as well as the extraordinary amortization established in article 43 of that law. 

Article 56.-Without prejudice to their right to utilize, in conformity with law, the normal or extraordinary systems for amortization of their remaining assets, the industrial enterprises in category "A" only may amortize, in conformity with the special system outlined in article 55 of this law, their investments in machinery and accessories, the importation of which is authorized in the corresponding Ministerial Accord, or which has been bought in the country with the prior knowledge and authorization of the Ministry of Economy. 

In either case, for consideration of the application which the enterprises present for the purpose of utilizing this system of amortization, the Bureau of Income Tax shall require the presentation of the certificate of the Ministry of Economy, to which the second paragraph of article 53 of this law refers, as to the authenticity of the acquisition and the price thereof, which certificate shall constitute sufficient proof of the value of those assets which should be amortized.

Article 57.-When a classified industrial enterprise sells all or part of the machinery or accessories which are being amortized, or have been totally amortized, any difference in favor of the enterprise between the price of sale and the depreciated value of the asset shall be considered as profit of the enterprise for tax purposes.

In the case described in the previous paragraph and without prejudice to the obligation to obtain the authorization of the Ministry of Economy when necessary, notice to the Bureau of Income Tax shall be a prerequisite for the sale of the aforementioned assets. 

In the event of bankruptcy or liquidation of an enterprise, the receiver must give the notice to which the previous paragraph refers.

Article 58.-Besides the items which are deductible from profits by virtue of article 39 of the Income Tax Law, classified industrial enterprises, whatever be their legal form, may also effect the following deductions: a. Proven expenses for technical research and professional training of personnel; b. Gifts or contributions, in money or kind, made to technical research or professional training centers which have juridical personality and are located within the country.

Aside from the other requirements demanded by law in order to take the deductions, those mentioned in the previous paragraph may not be taken without the presentation of a certificate from the Directorate General of Industries and Commerce, in which there shall be ascertained that the expenses have actually been incurred in technical research or professional training and shall establish the accepted amount of such expenses, including the donations or contributions treated in paragraph (b) of this article.

CHAPTER VII Controls and Sanctions 

Article 59.-Without prejudice to the other functions committed by law to the Directorate General of Industries and Commerce of the Ministry of Economy, it is its responsibility to insure the faithful observance of the obligations contracted by the industrial enterprises which enjoy the benefits granted by this law, for which purpose it must carry out, timely and diligently, the following functions: a. Maintain a register of values of the machinery, accessories, and spare parts imported with tax concessions by the classified industrial enterprises, with such detail needed for their identification; b. Prove compliance with the times for installation and effective production determined by the corresponding Ministerial Accord; verifying at the same time the existence of the machinery, accessories, and spare parts, if appropriate, the importation of which has been benefited with tax concessions. For recording observance with the times, the Directorate General of Industries and Commerce must prepare the corresponding affidavits (actas); c. Carry out investigations as to imported raw material, proving its utilization by unit of manufactured product; d. Carry out a periodic examination of the characteristics and operating conditions of the industry which serve as the bases for its classification; e. Proceed with the inspections and studies necessary to classify the technical and administrative efficiency of the enterprise for the purposes of articles 40, 41, and 42 of this law; f. Carry out the inspections and investigations which the Ministry of Economy judges necessary in order to authorize the transfers of ownership or use and the changes of destination of the machinery, accessories, and spare parts of the "classified" industries, and to issue, when appropriate, the certificates to which article 53 of this law refers; g. Prove the authenticity of the data contained in the application which the promoters of share companies or general partnerships present to the Minister of Economy, in order to obtain the benefits which article 48 of this law grants for the organization of these kinds of companies; h. Report when there are mergers or splits of enterprises, a sale, cession, transfer, and a change of denomination or firm name of an enterprise, and the establishment of subsidiary or related affiliated enterprises, in conformity with that provided in articles 38 and 39 of this law; and, i. Carry out all other inspections and investigations which shall be necessary for the proper application of this law.

Article 60.-The Ministries of Economy and of the Treas ury, with the previous report of the National Board of Economic Planning and Coordination, which may be issued at the Board's own initiative, may, by Ministerial 

Accord, withdraw or suspend all or part of the benefits enjoyed by an industrial enterprise in conformity with 158

Industrial Encouragement Law this law, without prejudice to the sanctions it may have incurred, when the following is proved: a. Willful misstatements in the data and information presented in the industrial development applications; b. Willful misstatements in the declarations which the enterprise must submit for tax purposes and in the books of account presented for purposes of checking or auditing; c. Failure to comply with the submission of periodic or occasional information requested by the Ministries of Economy and of the Treasury and the National Board of Economic Planning and Coordination; as well as the utilization of procedures to partially or totally impede the inspections and investigations of these bodies, or recourse to measures of whatever kind to induce errors on the part of public entities or officials; d. The loan, lease, exchange or sale of all or part of the machinery, accessories and spare parts, the importation of which has enjoyed the concessions in the payment of customs duties and consular fees which this law establishes, when the transfer of ownership or use takes place without the authorization of the Ministry of Economy within the period of time to which article 32 of this law refers; e. Subornation or attempt to suborn public officials with which the enterprises have relations in conformity with this law, without prejudice to the penalties determined in the Penal Code; f. Recourse to illicit means to impede the establishment of competitive enterprises or the use of methods at variance with fair competition of price and quality in order to obstruct their operations; g. The establishment of mergers of enterprises or the concerted agreement between enterprises with regard to policies of production, price, and distribution, when such mergers and agreements are prejudicial to the consumers in_particular, or to the national interests in general; h. The fixing of prices at levels harmful to consumers, which suppose the existence of excessive profits; i. Noncompliance with the quality norms established by the Ministry of Economy; and, j. Repeated noncompliance with the employer obligations of the enterprise.

Article 61.-Without prejudice to that provided in the pertinent laws as to accounting, the industries which enjoy the benefits of this law must keep, in a regular manner and to the full satisfaction of the Ministries of Economy and of the Treasury, a system for cost accounting and an accounting register in which to note, with the necessary detail, the movement of the reserve account for depreciation and amortization of their separate assets.

New industrial enterprises shall have a period of 3 years after the initiation of effective production to organize their system of cost accounting, without prejudice to the lesser period fixed in the scale of article 35 for the reconsideration of the tax concessions to new industries in category "C." The register of amortizations must be opened as soon as the enterprise begins the amortization of its assets.

Noncompliance with the obligations to which the previous paragraph refers shall be sufficient cause for the Ministries of Economy and of the Treasury to suspend the enterprise in the enjoyment of the benefits conferred by this law, until such time as noncompliance ceases. 

The Ministry of the Treasury shall regulate the form in which the aforementioned accounts must be kept. 

Article 62.-All machinery and accessories, for the importation of which tax benefits are granted in the corresponding Ministerial Accord, except for force majeure or fortuitous circumstance, must be actually imported within the term provided in the said Ministerial Accord. 

If this importation is not accomplished, the enterprise shall lose the right to enjoy those benefits for that part which corresponds to the machinery and accessories not imported within the term fixed. 

Article 63.—If the industrial enterprise does not begin its effective production within the term fixed in the corresponding Ministerial Accord, for reasons which the Ministry of Economy does not consider justified, said Ministerial Accord shall be considered null and the industrial enterprise must pay to the fiscal authority and to entities participating in Government revenues an amount equivalent to that which they have not received by reason of the tax concessions which the enterprise has effectively enjoyed in conformity with this law. 

Article 64.—If the industrial enterprise loans, leases, exchanges, or sells all or part of the machinery, accessories, and spare parts, for the importation of which it has enjoyed the tax concessions which this law establishes, and these transfers of ownership or use are effected without authorization of the Ministry of Economy during the term to which article 32 of this law refers, the enterprise shall have to pay to the fiscal authority and to the entities participating in Government revenues a quantity equivalent of three times that which they did not receive by reason of the customs duty concessions on the importation of the machinery, accessories, and spare parts, the ownership or use of which has been transferred. The enterprise will become subject to an equal sanction when, without authorization of the Ministry of Economy, it employs in a use other than that declared in the application, the machinery, accessories, and spare parts imported with the benefits which this law establishes, without prejudice to the other effects which may be brought about by this change, in conformity with article 34 of this law. 

Article 65.—If the sale of all or part of the machinery and accessories, the acquisition of which was considered as a reinvestment of profits, an investment effected through credit or an increase of capital for the purposes of the tax concessions indicated in article 52, is effected without the knowledge or authorization of the Ministry of Economy within the 2 years following that in which the deduction took place, the enterprise, by this act alone, shall be subject to the sanctions provided in article 3 of the regulation for the application of subsection (j) of article 39 of the Income Tax Law, without prejudice to the other sanctions which this law establishes for these sales without authorization of the Ministry of Economy. 

Article 66.—If the industrial enterprises which enjoy the benefits granted by virtue of this law engage in any of the violations established in subsections (a), (b), (c), (e) and (f) of article 60, in addition to the withdrawal or suspension of all or part of these benefits, the Ministry of Economy and that of the Treasury must impose the fine which they consider necessary, from 1,000 to 

100,000 sucres, in conformity with the gravity of the violation or the repetition of the same, and the economic capacity of the enterprise.

CHAPTER VIII General Provisions 

Article 67.—In the event of sale, cession, transfer, change of denomination or firm name of a classified enterprise, merger of enterprises in which there are one or more classified enterprises, their split or any other change as a consequence of which the benefits established by this law may be enjoyed by enterprises or persons other than those orginally favored, the Ministry of Economy and that of the Treasury, with prior notice of this change, must decide if the industry shall continue to be considered as classified for the purposes of this law. If there is no notice when one of the listed changes is made, the original Ministerial Accord shall be tacitly revoked. 159

In the event of bankruptcy or liquidation of an industrial enterprise, the receiver must inform the Ministry of Economy of this fact for purposes of the decision to which the previous paragraph refers.

Article 68.-When, because of competition from foreign products, the enjoyment of the tax concessions granted by this law are insufficient to stimulate the normal development of industrial enterprises in categories "A" and "B" which are considered of vital importance for the development of the economy of the country, the Ministry of Economy, after consultation with the National Board of 

Economic Planning and Coordination, shall request of the 

Ministry of the Treasury those reforms in the Customs Tariff which are deemed advisable with regard to the amount of the duties on imports of the foreign product or products which compete with the national article. 

In order to request the reforms in the Customs Tariff to which the previous paragraph refers, the Ministry of Economy must have as its objective the establishment of a level of protection indispensable for stimulating only those industrial enterprises which operate with the maximum technical and administrative efficiency compatible with the conditions of the economy of the country. 

Article 69.-When a classified industry begins to produce raw material, semimanufactured products or intermediate manufactured products which may be utilized in the industrial process of national enterprises or in other productive activities, in substitution of similar foreign ones, provided that their use does not affect the technical and economic efficiency of these enterprises to the prejudice of the national interest, no importations of these similar ones may be effected without the express authorization of the Ministry of Economy, which shall be granted only when the placement of national production has been fully assured.

The Ministry of Economy shall condition its decisions in this area on the compliance, on the part of the beneficiary enterprise, with the quality norms and the provisions as to prices which shall be deemed advisable to issue in defense of the national interest.

The resolutions which the Ministry of Economy issues by virtue of that provided in this article are appealable by the interested parties to the Foreign Commerce Council (Consejo de Comercio Exterior), which must resolve the corresponding applications within a period of 15 days after the date of their presentation. The resolutions of the Ministry of Economy shall be executed if the interested parties do not interpose an appeal procedure within a term of 8 days after the date on which these resolutions were officially notified.

Article 70.-The classified enterprises shall contribute to the expenses of the investigations and to the costs of the inspections and checks which must be made by the Direc torate General of Industries and Commerce of the Min istry of Economy and the National Board of Economic Planning and Coordination for the correct application of this law, with 3 percent of the sums which the fiscal authority and the entities participating in Government revenues shall have failed to receive as a result of the tax concessions with which the enterprise has been benefited in conformity with this law, excepting those which result from the right to compensate losses and the recourse to the special system for accelerated amortization.

The Ministry of the Treasury must effect payment of this contribution before delivering to the interested party the documents for the withdrawal of the machinery, accessories, and spare parts, for which the import duties have been excused or reduced, and before computing annually the taxes affected by the provisions of chapter VI of this law. When dealing with tax concessions on the importation of raw material, if the enterprise has elected the system described in subsection (1) of article 30, the Ministry of the Treasury must deduct the corresponding percentage of the amount for which the credit note is issued; or shall proceed to the collection of said contribution when the exonerations are made effective, if the enterprise has elected the system provided in subsection 2 of that article.

The amounts derived from these contributions, as well as the fines imposed by virtue of article 66, shall be credited to a special account entitled "Expenses for Ad ministration of the Industrial Development Law," which shall have two subaccounts, namely: "Directorate General of Industries and Commerce-Ministry of Economy," to which two-thirds of the contributions shall be credited, and "National Board of Economic Planning and Coordination," which shall be increased with one-third of the contributions. The sums credited shall be made to the order of the agencies named on the subaccounts, and shall be invested with simple authorization of the Ministry of the Treasury.

Article 71.-The Industrial Protection Law of 1921 and its amendments, the Legislative Decree of December 18, 1946, relative to tax exonerations for annual increases in production, as well as all laws and decrees which are in conflict with this law are repealed, with the exception of the Legislative Decree of November 2, 1949, relative to the computation of the tax on the sales of industrial enterprises, the special laws which govern the activities indicated in article 4 of this law and the Legislative Decree of October 30, 1954, which grants a special tax treatment to the new industries of the Provinces of Azuay and Cañar.

The repealers of the previous paragraph shall not affect the rights and obligations of the contracts for industrial protection in effect for which there has not been an express renunciation. 

Article 72.-Notwithstanding that provided in article 71, the new industries which, in the future, shall be established in the Provinces of Azuay and Cañar may adopt, if they choose, the regime for industrial development of this law, but in such case they may not enjoy the special treatment enacted by the Legislative Decree of October 30, 1954.

Nevertheless, there shall be extended to the new industries established or to be established under the protection of the law of October 30, 1954, according to the category which corresponds to them, the concessions on customs duties and consular fees for the importation of machinery, accessories, spare parts, and raw materials, and the benefit as to the manner of computing the term for the enjoyment of the exonerations, under the terms provided in this law. The industries established under the protection of the special law for Azuay and Cañar shall automatically come under the regime which this law grants to existing industries, at the termination of the time periods of the contracts celebrated in conformity with that special law. 

Article 73.-In a period of 60 days from the date of the promulgation of this law, the Ministry of Economy shall issue the corresponding regulations.

Article 74.-The Ministries of Economy and of the Treas ury are empowered to decide and regulate those unfore seen cases which arise from the administration of this law.

Article 75.-This law shall be effective from the date of its publication in the Registro Oficial. 

Transitory Provisions 

Article 76. The industries already established which enjoy industrial protection contracts granted in conform ity with Industrial Protection Law of 1921, repealed by this law, and which are in a position to benefit from the regime established in this law, may not enjoy the same without the prior renunciation, on the part of the state and the enterprise, of the rights and duties established by such contracts. 160

Industrial Encouragement Law 

In the situation of the previous paragraph, in the application which is presented to the Ministry of Economy for coming under the benefits of this law there must be included a promise of renunciation of the industrial protection contracts, which renunciation shall be a prerequisite for the issuance of the Ministerial Accord. 

Article 77.-The industries which are in the circumstances referred to in the previous article and which, prior to the renunciation of the rights and duties of the industrial protection contracts, figure as classified ones by reason of the corresponding Ministerial Accord, shall be eligible to enjoy the tax concessions provided in articles 35 and 36 of this law, when the period during which they are in effective production is less than the maximum number of years for which these concessions are extended for the category within which the industry has been classified.

In the situation of the previous paragraph, the industry may enjoy the tax concessions for the number of years which are lacking for completion of the maximum period of protection assigned to each category and, in such a case, with reference to the benefits established in article 35 of this law, the concessions shall begin with the percentages corresponding to the number of years which the enterprise has been in effective production, in conformity with the corresponding scale. With regard to the possibility of deducting losses, to which article 36 refers, the enterprise shall have this right during the number of years which remains of the period provided for each category.

When applicable, in conformity with the scale and procedures of the articles cited, the enterprises must also request that the Ministry of Economy classify them as efficient or inefficient for the corresponding purposes. 

The definition of the number of years provided in article 37 of this law is also valid for the application of this article. In case of doubt, the Directorate General of Industries and Commerce of the Ministry of Economy shall fix the date which must be considered as the initiation of the effective production of the enterprise. 

Article 78.-When existing industries, the effective production of which was initiated in years prior to the date of enactment of this law, present an application for relief from the ruinous competition of new enterprises, so as to come under the system of concessions provided in articles 43 and 44, if the application is accepted, the determination of the coefficients for reduction applicable to the petitioning enterprise shall not be subject to the limits established in subsections I and II of section (d) of article 44, and these coefficients, if the Ministry of Economy and the Ministry of the Treasury consider it appropriate, may be up to 100 percent of the coefficients for reduction which apply for the new enterprise which is the subject of the application. 

Article 79.-Existing classified industries may only adopt the special system of amortization established in article 55 of this law for their investments in machinery and accessories, when they begin to amortize a new investment in such capital assets. 

Article 80.-The industrial enterprises existing on the date of the enactment of this law have a period of 2 years after that date to organize the system of accounting for costs to which article 61 of this law refers. 

Article 81. So long as a special legal regime for the electric power producing enterprises is not issued, such enterprises, provided that their establishment and operation conform to the plans and dispositions of the National Board of Economic Planning and Coordination, may be eligible for the benefits which this law grants to industries in category "A," modified, in conformity with the peculiar character of such enterprises, by the regulation which the Ministries of Economy and of the Treasury, with the prior favorable report of the National Board of 

Economic Planning and Coordination, shall issue for this purpose.

Article 82. For all legal purposes, there shall be considered as new industries all those which have not begun their effective production on the date of the enactment of this law.

Article 83.-The Ministries of Economy and Treasury may process and decide, prior to the issuance of the regulations to this law, the applications for industrial development for the establishment of new industrial enterprises, when it is deemed that the delay may be prejudicial to the enterprise and the national interest, and the importance of the industry justifies it. 

In the situation of the previous paragraph, the instructions of these Ministries shall establish the procedures which must be followed; but all of the decisions taken shall be of a provisional character and shall only be definitive if they are not expressly modified in the month following the issuance of the regulations. 

Article 84. The concessions in the payment of the customs duties and consular fees established in chapter V of this law may be extended to those importations, for the classified industrial enterprises, of machinery, accessories, spare parts, and, when appropriate, of raw material, for which orders were placed before the enactment of this law, without such importations having arrived in national territory by that date.

The Ministries of Economy and of the Treasury shall determine the list of machinery, accessories, spare parts, and, when appropriate, raw material, which, under the conditions of the previous paragraph, may be covered by the concessions in the payment of import duties. 

Article 85.-After 1 year from the date of promulgation of this law, the periods to which articles 19 and 21 thereof refer shall be increased to 3 and 6 months respectively, except for those corresponding to applications for industrial development for the installation of new industries which will continue under those provided in the cited articles.

Article 86. During a term of 3 years from the date of the enactment of this law, in defense of the national interest and to prevent adverse effects which may produce unforeseen circumstances, the Ministries of Economy and Treasury are empowered to modify the percentages which represent the value of the imported raw material in the cost of production or in the price of exportation, and which shall serve as criteria of classification. This modification shall always require the prior favorable report of the National Council of Economic 

Planning and Coordination.

Article 87.-The Ministries of Economy and of the Treas ury are charged with the execution of this decree. 

Issued at the National Palace, in Quito, on the 21st of June 1957. 161

APPENDIX B Entry and Customs Regulations, Government Representation ENTRY REGULATIONS Required Documents 

Tourists, commercial travelers, and resident aliens entering Ecuador are required to have the following documents: (1) Passport, (2) health certificate, and (3) smallpox vaccination certificate.

Other Documents, by Purpose of Entry 

Additional requirements vary in accordance with the purpose of the entry. 

Tourists. Tourists who are citizens of other Western

Hemisphere countries may enter Ecuador with tourist cards in place of visas. Tourist cards may be obtained from Ecuadoran consular offices or authorized travel agencies upon payment of a $1 fee and upon presentation of the 3 required documents plus 2 passport-size photographs. 

Tourist cards are valid for entry for 90 days from date of issue and authorize tourists to remain in the country for 90 days from date of entry. Ecuadoran immigration authorities are empowered to extend the stay an additional 90 days, upon request. The tourist card also serves as an exit permit or visa.

Holders of tourist cards are not authorized to engage in any type of business or other remunerative activity or to change their tourist status while in Ecuador. 

Transients.-Travelers passing in transit through Ecuador are not required to have visas or tourist cards if they remain in the country less than 24 hours. Transients intending to remain longer should have tourist cards. 

Commercial travelers.-Persons traveling to Ecuador for business reasons are required to have business visas, which are issued without fee to United States citizens upon personal application to an Ecuadoran consular office.

The application for a business visa must be accompanied by the following, in addition to the 3 required documents: (1) 4 passport-size photographs, and (2) a round-trip ticket or a ticket for onward transportation with appropriate documentation for entry into country of next destination. In lieu of a ticket, the traveler may present a letter of guaranty from his employer or bank. 

Business visas are valid for entry for 90 days from date of issue and authorize holders to remain in Ecuador for 90 days from date of entry. The immigration authorities are empowered to grant an extension of an additional 90 days upon request. 

Travelers entering Ecuador on business are required to report to the Ecuadoran immigration authorities upon or soon after arrival, and prior to departure they must obtain an exit visa from the authorities. The exit visa is issued without charge. Business visitors are not permitted to change their status while in Ecuador. 

Resident aliens.-Persons entering Ecuador to reside permanently or for an indefinite or extended time are required to have immigration visas, which may be obtained by application through Ecuadoran consular offices or direct to the Board of Immigration (Junta de Immigracion) of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. If the application is accepted, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs will authorize the appropriate consular office to issue an immigration visa upon payment of a $25 fee and presentation of the following, in addition to the 3 required documents: (1) 4 passport-size photographs, (2) police certificate attesting to the good conduct of the applicant, (3) character references, (4) a deposit of $500. The last requirement may be waived by specific authorization from the Board of Immigration, or it may be satisfied by a copy of the contract under which the applicant will be employed in Ecuador.

Holders of immigration visas are required to report to the Ecuadoran immigration authorities after their arrival in the country.

A resident alien of Ecuador who has been absent from the country for less than 1 year may be granted a return visa upon presentation of the following, in addition to the 3 required documents: (1) 4 passport-size photographs, (2) residence identification card or a letter from the Board of Immigration authorizing issuance of the visa. Charges for this service vary in accordance with the terms of the special agreements which Ecuador has with the other countries. The fee for United States citizens is $10.

CUSTOMS TREATMENT 

Temporary visitors are not required to pay import duties on used personal articles, provided the type and quantity of such articles are considered commensurate with the status of the traveler and the length of his intended visit. Included among such nondutiable personal effects are clothing, books, sports equipment, musical instruments, photographic equipment, and a maximum of 163

cigars, 300 cigarettes, 200 grams of pipe tobacco, and 1 liter of wine or liquor. Travelers may also include in their luggage up to $125 worth of dutiable goods on which payment of duty is not required. There are no limitations on the amount of dollars that may be brought into Ecuador.

Travelers entering Ecuador with immigration visas are entitled to duty-free entry of many of their household effects, such as toilet articles, linen, tableware and kitchenware, firearms, typewriter, and such household appliances as a refrigerator, washing machine, kitchen stove, vacuum cleaner, and radio. To qualify for duty exemption, household appliances must show obvious signs of Furniture is not exempt from the payment of import duties. Tools and instruments used by an immigrant in his work are admitted duty free. use.

Automobiles may be brought into Ecuador by tourists free of duty. Tourists are required, however, to post a bond with customs for the amount of the applicable import duty. The bond is canceled on reexportation of the vehicle. The bond requirement does not apply to vehicles entering Ecuador by road from Colombia. Apparently this procedure has not been standardized, as there have been instances of motorists entering the country without having to post a bond. Uncertainties and difficulties in the procedure can usually be obviated through membership in an automobile club with inter-American associations.

Immigrants bringing automobiles into Ecuador are required to pay the applicable import duty.

APPENDIX C U. S. Companies Operating in Ecuador

This list includes only those concerns in which United States firms or individuals have a substantial direct capital investment in the form of stock, as the sole owner, or as a partner in the enterprise. Noncommercial enterprises and institutions, such as churches, missions, schools, and hospitals, financed or operated by American charitable or religious organizations, have also been omitted.

The list was compiled for the U. S. Department of Commerce by the American Embassy in Quito.

Every effort has been made to include only firms of good repute, but the Department can assume no responsibility in connection with any actions conducted with such persons or firms. Moreover, the list does not purport to be authoritative as to legal status or relationship existing between the United States firms and individuals involved and their business enterprises in Ecuador.

WORLD TRADE INFORMATION SERVICE 

The World Trade Information Service, which keeps these country handbooks up to date, is a three-part series of reports published by the Bureau of 

Foreign Commerce.

Each WTIS report covers a particular country or area and deals with a subject of current interest to traders and investors. 

Subscriptions may be placed with Department of Commerce Field Offices or with the Superintendent of Documents, U. S. Government Printing Office, Washington 25, D. C. Rates are quoted below.

PART 1, ECONOMIC REPORTS 

Economic and marketing data-basic information on the general economy-current economic developments-transportation-business organization-investment laws and policies. $6 a year (foreign, $9.75). 

PART 2, OPERATIONS REPORTS 

Trading aids-import tariff system-licensing and exchange controlsshipping documents, labeling, marking-food, pharmaceutical, patent, trademark regulations-living costs and conditions. $6 a year (foreign, $11). 

PART 3, STATISTICAL REPORTS 

Trade statistics for market research-monthly total U. S. export-import trade U. S. trade with major countries or areas-foreign trade of individual countries-world trade reviews. $6 a year (foreign, $8.50). 

U. S. Department of Commerce Field Offices are located in Albuquerque, N. Mex.; Atlanta, Ga.; 

Boston, Mass.; Buffalo, N. Y.; Charleston, S. C.; Cheyenne, Wyo.; Chicago, Ill.; Cincinnati, Ohio; 

Cleveland, Ohio; Dallas, Tex.; Denver, Colo.; Detroit, Mich.; Greensboro, N. C.; Houston, Tex.; 

Jacksonville, Fla.; Kansas City, Mo.; Los Angeles, Calif.; Memphis, Tenn.; Miami, Fla.; Minneapolis, Minn.; New Orleans, La.; New York, N. Y.; Philadelphia, Pa.; Phoenix, Ariz.; Pittsburgh, Pa.; 

Portland, Oreg.; Reno, Nev.; Richmond, Va.; St. Louis, Mo.; Salt Lake City, Utah; San Francisco,

Calif.; Savannah, Ga.; and Seattle, Wash. For addresses and telephone numbers see U. S. Government section of telephone book.
